I came in today because, well, even on my better days, I just can't seem to shake this feeling I've been having. It seems like some people at my church and in my walking club are watching me. I keep noticing that they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or hiding things about me in what they say when I'm around. It's just been bothering me a lot.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices or sounds that other people around you don't seem to hear?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually, I have noticed that a bit. I came in today because, well, even on my better days, I just can't seem to shake this feeling I've been having. It seems like some people at my church and in my walking club are watching me. I keep noticing that they're putting little messages about me in the church bulletin, or hiding things about me in what they say when I'm around. It's just been bothering me a lot.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices or sounds that other people around you don't seem to hear?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually, I have noticed that a bit. Sometimes when the house is just quiet, I'll hear my name being called, or maybe just a few words. It sounds exactly like my friend's voice coming from the kitchen, or out on the porch. It's so clear that I'll actually answer back out loud... and then I remember that I'm home alone and nobody is actually there.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these feelings of being watched and hearing voices? Did these start suddenly over days, or have they been gradually building up over weeks or months?\n\nPatient: Well, it didn't happen all at once. I'd say the voices, those little messages, and... well, just having trouble keeping my thoughts straight sometimes, it's all been happening for about eleven months now. So I suppose it built up gradually over that time.\n\nDoctor: During these eleven months, have there been any stretches of time—maybe a few weeks or longer—where you felt completely back to normal, with no voices, no sense of being watched, and your thoughts felt clear again?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think there's been any time like that. It's just been pretty constant the whole eleven months. I haven't had a break where everything just went back to normal.\n\nDoctor: During these eleven months when you've been hearing voices and feeling watched, have there been times when your mood was extremely elevated or energetic for several days in a row, or times when you felt very depressed or hopeless for extended periods?\n\nPatient: Well, I've never felt overly energetic or anything like that. But I have had times where I felt very depressed. There was a stretch where I was at my absolute lowest. I was barely eating, and I just kept thinking I had failed everyone in my life. And during that same time, I was still hearing my friend's voice calling out to me, and I was still thinking the people from church were sending those messages about me. So, the voices and the low mood, they were happening at the exact same time.\n\nDoctor: When you think back to the beginning of those eleven months, did you start hearing the voices and feeling like people were watching you before you began feeling very depressed, or did the depression come first?\n\nPatient: Well, the voices and the feeling that people were talking about me definitely came first. I had that going on for a while before the depression really hit me hard. And actually, there was a spell, maybe for a few weeks, where my mood lifted a bit. I was getting dressed every day, eating a little better, and just not feeling so weighed down by everything. But even during that better time, I was still hearing my name being called in the house, and I still thought the people at church were dropping hints about me. So the voices and the paranoia, they just kept going even when the depression wasn't as bad.\n\nDoctor: When you first started noticing the voices and feeling like people were talking about you, before the depression hit hard, were you experiencing any other changes—like problems sleeping, changes in your appetite, or difficulty concentrating—or was it mainly just the voices and the sense that people were watching you?\n\nPatient: Well, when it first started, it was mostly just the voices and feeling like people were watching me. But, um, my appetite did change. Most days, food just doesn't appeal to me at all. I mostly just pick at some toast or maybe a little soup. I've definitely noticed my skirts have been hanging a lot looser than they used to, so I know I've lost some weight. But the sleep and concentrating... I don't really remember those being a big issue back then, just the eating and the weight loss.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned that the voices and the feeling that people were watching you started first, and then later you had that period of depression, and then a few weeks where you felt a bit better. During those weeks when your mood lifted and you were getting dressed and eating better, did the voices and the feeling that people were talking about you become quieter or less frequent, or did they stay just as strong as before?\n\nPatient: Well, they didn't really go away. They stayed about the same, honestly. But, um, if I look back at this whole eleven months... for most of this whole stretch, the sadness, the guilt, and just not caring about anything anymore... they've been there in the background almost the whole time. Even when the voices or those thoughts about people at church were a little quieter, that heavy, sad feeling was still just... hanging over me.\n\nDoctor: When you think back to those first few weeks or months when the voices and the feeling that people were watching you started, before the depression came on—roughly how many weeks or months would you say the voices and paranoia were going on before you started feeling that heavy sadness and guilt?\n\nPatient: Well, I think I might have confused things a bit earlier. When I really think about it... the deep sadness, the guilt, and just losing interest in everything... that's actually been going on for about a year and two months now. It's been there for a long time.\n\nDoctor: I want to make sure I have the timeline right. When you look back to the very beginning—before any of the sadness and guilt started—were you hearing your friend's voice calling your name and noticing those messages from church people for a clear period of time, even when your mood felt relatively normal?\n\nPatient: Well, actually, it was kind of the other way around. The sadness has been there the longest. But there was a time... a spell of several weeks where the sadness lifted a bit. I was getting dressed, eating a bit better, and just not feeling so weighed down by it all. But even during that time, when my mood felt okay, I still heard my friend's voice calling my name in the house, and I still thought the people at church were dropping hints about me. So the voices and the thoughts didn't stop, even when the depression took a break.\n\nDoctor: I want to clarify something important. Right now, today, are you still hearing your friend's voice calling your name and still feeling like people at church are sending you messages about you—even though your mood feels relatively stable at the moment?\n\nPatient: Yes, I am. I still hear my friend calling my name today, and I still feel like those messages are happening at church. \n\nBut I want to make sure I'm explaining it right, because it's not just when my mood is stable. So the sadness was already there for a few months before the voices and messages started.\n\nDoctor: Right now, today, when you think about your mood—would you say you're feeling depressed, or would you say your mood feels relatively normal or okay?\n\nPatient: Right now, today...
